5217: 8.2    Ward identities and Euclidean covariance
5218: Theorem 8.16 (Fixed-group invariant-jet admission through dimension six). For a compact simple Lie
5219: algebra g:
5220: (I1) the invariant symmetric bilinear forms form a one-dimensional space;
5221: (I2) there is no reflection-compatible gauge-invariant local vector of engineering dimension at most four;
5222: (I3) modulo total differences, equations of motion, and the reflection-odd topological density, the reflection-
5223:      even scalar dimension-four block is generated by the Yang–Mills density;
5224: (I4) the gauge-invariant jet quotient through dimension six is finite. A primitive cubic symmetric color
5225:      tensor occurs precisely for Lie type Ar with r ≥ 2 and is retained in the dimension-six block; its
5226:       presence does not enlarge the dimension-four marginal block.
5227: The filtered finite-matrix transport and associated-graded contraction used below therefore apply for every
5228: fixed G after enlarging only the finite jet dimension.
5229: Proof. Simplicity gives the uniqueness of the invariant bilinear form. Semisimplicity makes every finite-
5230: dimensional representation traceless on g. Lorentz index counting, gauge covariance, and reflection parity
5231: then exclude a single free vector index through dimension four and leave only the quadratic curvature
5232: scalar in the even dimension-four sector. Chevalley’s theorem makes the invariant polynomial algebra
5233: finitely generated; the degrees are the exponents plus one. Through degree three a primitive symmetric
5234: tensor exists only for type Ar , and it first multiplies three curvatures, hence has engineering dimension
5235: six. At every fixed engineering cutoff only finitely many covariant derivatives and invariant contractions
5236: occur.

3259: Lemma 5.23 (No reflection-even rank-two curvature jet of dimension five). In the local gauge-invariant
3260: rank-two curvature-jet quotient relevant to the stress/displacement source, the reflection-even engineering-
3261: dimension-five component is zero.
3262: Proof. A dimension-five curvature bilinear has the schematic Lorentz-index content F DF , hence five
3263: tensor indices before contractions. To leave exactly two free rank-two indices one would have to contract
3264: an odd set of three indices. The signed-hypercubic invariant tensors available in the reflection-even
3265: sector are products of Kronecker deltas and pairs of Levi–Civita tensors, all of even rank. A single
3266: Levi–Civita tensor is reflection odd. Thus no reflection-even constant coefficient tensor can perform the
3267: required contraction. Terms in which the derivative is moved onto the whole bilinear are total differences,
3268: while Bianchi and equation-of-motion descendants are already quotiented. The next possible rank-two
3269: complement after dimension four therefore begins at dimension six.

4786: score as the exterior root; for an admitted optical chart interpolation the affine derivative inserts the
4787: corresponding registered optical score. The marked row is uniform in the exterior data allowed by the

4803: Lemma 7.5 (No massless retained reference at matching). In the exact post-compact joint partition of
4804: Corollary 6.40, the retained compact field has product normalized Haar reference and has already been

4808: in the explicit inventory AJG .
4809: Proof. The exact compact collect of Theorem 6.39 and the post-compact identity of Corollary 6.40 restore
4810: product Haar on the retained compact variables and place every nonempty retained dependence into the
4811: compact, Gaussian, regular, or irregular activity inventories. The weak tangent Gaussian contributes
4812: no undeclared covariance edge to this matching logarithm. By contrast, the optical one-core references
4813: remain live, while their off-diagonal quadratic blocks and other cross-core terms occur explicitly in AJG
4814: and retain the spatial payment used in Theorem 6.43.
4815: Lemma 7.6 (Vacuum factorization after the complete owner map). Fix the optical variables and expand
4816: one compact coefficient in Peter–Weyl sectors. Suppose every compact cut separating two declared root
4817: buffers carries the trivial representation. Remove only the cross-core objects already registered in the
4818: four disjoint inventories of Corollary 6.40. If no registered connector remains between the roots, the
4819: residual coefficient factorizes into one-core expectations and empty-support scalars. Its contribution to the
4820: normalized connected two-root logarithm is zero.
4821: Proof. Triviality on every separating compact cut permits repeated Haar–Fubini separation. The exact
4822: incidence-split collect retains every internally nontrivial singlet in AJH , while the owner map assigns every
4823: other cross-core occurrence to exactly one of AJG , AJreg , AJirr , or AJH . After those registered objects are
4824: removed, the residual history has no object joining distinct one-core components. It therefore factorizes.
4825: Empty-support scalars are removed by the normalization, and products of separate one-root expectations
4826: are removed by the connected logarithm.
4827: Theorem 7.7 (Exact acoustic exclusion-or-capture). Consider any connected zero-, one-, or two-source
4828: coefficient joining two bounded matching-scale root buffers in the exact joint logarithm. At least one object
4829: on the unique root-to-root forest path belongs to one of the following classes:
4830:    (i) a nonvacuum compact activity controlled by Theorem 6.39 and the spatial row (99);
4831:   (ii) an optical covariance bond or an optical Gaussian cross-core activity in AJG ;
4832:  (iii) a registered reference, regular, irregular, seam, reflection, wrapping, normalization, determinant, or
4833:        source connector with its original support payment.
4834: There is no fourth class consisting of a massless retained acoustic reference edge.
4835: Proof. Assume that a connected coefficient contains no object of types (i)–(iii). Absence of (i) makes
4836: every separating compact cut vacuum. Absence of (iii) leaves no registered physical connector after the
4837: complete owner map. Lemma 7.6 then reduces the compact contribution to disconnected one-core factors
4838: and scalars. Absence of (ii) also prevents the still-live optical field from joining the roots. The coefficient
4839: is therefore disconnected, a contradiction. The only possible remaining concern would be an undeclared
4840: massless retained covariance, which is excluded by Lemma 7.5.

4846: Proof. The vertex inventory is already exhaustive by Lemma 6.7, Proposition 6.8, and Corollary 6.40.

5217: 8.2    Ward identities and Euclidean covariance
5218: Theorem 8.16 (Fixed-group invariant-jet admission through dimension six). For a compact simple Lie
5219: algebra g:
5220: (I1) the invariant symmetric bilinear forms form a one-dimensional space;
5221: (I2) there is no reflection-compatible gauge-invariant local vector of engineering dimension at most four;
5222: (I3) modulo total differences, equations of motion, and the reflection-odd topological density, the reflection-
5223:      even scalar dimension-four block is generated by the Yang–Mills density;
5224: (I4) the gauge-invariant jet quotient through dimension six is finite. A primitive cubic symmetric color
5225:      tensor occurs precisely for Lie type Ar with r ≥ 2 and is retained in the dimension-six block; its
5226:       presence does not enlarge the dimension-four marginal block.
5227: The filtered finite-matrix transport and associated-graded contraction used below therefore apply for every
5228: fixed G after enlarging only the finite jet dimension.
5229: Proof. Simplicity gives the uniqueness of the invariant bilinear form. Semisimplicity makes every finite-
5230: dimensional representation traceless on g. Lorentz index counting, gauge covariance, and reflection parity
5231: then exclude a single free vector index through dimension four and leave only the quadratic curvature
5232: scalar in the even dimension-four sector. Chevalley’s theorem makes the invariant polynomial algebra
5233: finitely generated; the degrees are the exponents plus one. Through degree three a primitive symmetric
5234: tensor exists only for type Ar , and it first multiplies three curvatures, hence has engineering dimension
5235: six. At every fixed engineering cutoff only finitely many covariant derivatives and invariant contractions
5236: occur.

5070: physical neighborhood. Their ultraviolet contact subtrees are not summed as separate long connectors;
5071: they are contracted into the exact local atom described in the statement.

5158: and a fixed countable set of dyadic physical source scales, and the resulting subsequence is relabelled as
5159: the directed sequence appearing in Theorem 1.1; no uniqueness or schedule-independence claim is needed.
5160: In every connected partition joining two separated groups, at least one tree edge crosses the separation;
5161: factor its exponential and sum all remaining edges with (118). Full moments follow from the set-partition
5162: formula.

5188: Lemma 8.14 (Reflection positivity inheritance under exact recombination). Every limiting positive-time

5193: positivity passes to the common limiting family.

5205: proves the claim.
5206: Theorem 8.15 (Common positive-scale Schwinger system). The common subsequence defines tempered,
5207: permutation-symmetric, reflection-positive, continuously translation-covariant Schwinger distributions for
5208: the countable algebra generated by the bounded positive-scale observables and every fixed finite normalized
5209: local source family. The distributions are projectively compatible and satisfy uniform physical exponential
5210: clustering.
5211: Proof. The Schwartz estimate gives equicontinuity on each finite tensor power. Diagonal extraction over
5212: a countable dense test/source family gives the common projective system. Permutation symmetry passes
5213: as an algebraic identity, while Lemma 8.14 gives the complete reflection-positivity mechanism after exact
5214: label recombination. Riemann- sum translation errors vanish by the physical Schur bound, Corollary 8.12
5215: gives clustering, and Lemma 8.13 gives the OS linear-growth row.

5217: 8.2    Ward identities and Euclidean covariance
5218: Theorem 8.16 (Fixed-group invariant-jet admission through dimension six). For a compact simple Lie
5219: algebra g:
5220: (I1) the invariant symmetric bilinear forms form a one-dimensional space;
5221: (I2) there is no reflection-compatible gauge-invariant local vector of engineering dimension at most four;
5222: (I3) modulo total differences, equations of motion, and the reflection-odd topological density, the reflection-
5223:      even scalar dimension-four block is generated by the Yang–Mills density;
5224: (I4) the gauge-invariant jet quotient through dimension six is finite. A primitive cubic symmetric color
5225:      tensor occurs precisely for Lie type Ar with r ≥ 2 and is retained in the dimension-six block; its
5226:       presence does not enlarge the dimension-four marginal block.
5227: The filtered finite-matrix transport and associated-graded contraction used below therefore apply for every
5228: fixed G after enlarging only the finite jet dimension.
5229: Proof. Simplicity gives the uniqueness of the invariant bilinear form. Semisimplicity makes every finite-
5230: dimensional representation traceless on g. Lorentz index counting, gauge covariance, and reflection parity
5231: then exclude a single free vector index through dimension four and leave only the quadratic curvature
5232: scalar in the even dimension-four sector. Chevalley’s theorem makes the invariant polynomial algebra
5233: finitely generated; the degrees are the exponents plus one. Through degree three a primitive symmetric
5234: tensor exists only for type Ar , and it first multiplies three curvatures, hence has engineering dimension
5235: six. At every fixed engineering cutoff only finitely many covariant derivatives and invariant contractions
5236: occur.

5014: analytic coefficient of each exact conditional logarithm occurs exactly once. After absolute values are

5017: Proof. For one conditional integration, differentiation of the normalized logarithm is the joint-cumulant
5018: identity. The set-partition formula for a derivative of a composition is precisely the Banach-valued Faà di
5019: Bruno formula: every block of the partition is one incoming connected component and the outer cumulant
5020: joins those blocks at the current scale. Fixing the least-label order makes this representation unique.
5021: Induction over the finite sequence of exact conditional integrations proves (115); the terminal matching
5022: logarithm is the final induction step. The weak rooted margin pays every nonroot weak vertex by σ < 1,
5023: while the finite macrobridge and matching KP expansion pay the finitely many terminal vertices. The
5024: one/two-root bounds control a component while its source leaves remain external. These majorants are
5025: summable independently of m, so dominated convergence gives the last assertion.

5030: recursively generated tree, not a choice made after absolute values or bounds have been applied.

5035: irregular sectors are recombined. Every connected tree has exactly one lowest common ancestor for the two
5036: source leaves, and the recursive least-label convention gives it exactly one representation in Lemma 8.6.
5037: Descendants have strictly finer labels or belong to the same finite conditional vertex and are summed by
5038: its connected cumulant. Exact regular/irregular recombination precedes the conditional logarithm at every
5039: scale, so the forest formula expands the full measure rather than a conditioned all-good measure.

4993: Exact finite-depth multiscale forest formula For a microscopic depth m, let Rj denote the exact
4994: normalized conditional-logarithm map that integrates the scale-j fluctuation field and returns the source-
4995: decorated effective interaction at scale j − 1. The maps include the regular/irregular partition of unity
4996: before the logarithm is taken, so no all-good denominator occurs. Write
4997: 
4998:                                      Wm (J) = log Zm (J) − log Zm (0)

5000: for the normalized finite-volume source logarithm. Iterating the exact maps Rm , . . . , Rjw and then the
5001: finite macrobridge is an identity, not an asymptotic expansion.
5002:    A scale-labelled source forest is generated recursively as follows. A source derivative is a labelled leaf.
5003: At one conditional integration, connected cumulant differentiation joins a nonempty partition of the
5004: incoming leaves and decorated subtrees to one new vertex labelled by that integration scale. Terms
5005: transported without joining retain their existing components. At the terminal matching logarithm, the
5006: remaining components are joined by the matching-scale connected cumulants. The ordering of children is
5007: fixed by the least source label, so isomorphic terms are not counted more than once.
5008: Lemma 8.6 (Exact multiscale Faà di Bruno forest formula). For every finite depth m and every fixed
5009: derivative order n,                                        X
5010:                              Dn Wm (0)[J1 , . . . , Jn ] =   Am (τ ; J1 , . . . , Jn ),                 (115)
5011:                                                       τ ∈Tm,n
5012: 
5013: where Tm,n is the finite family of recursively generated scale-labelled connected trees described above. Each
5014: analytic coefficient of each exact conditional logarithm occurs exactly once. After absolute values are
5015: taken, the sum is dominated uniformly in m by the rooted weak rows and the finite matching expansion.
5016: Hence (115) passes to the infinite weak-depth limit termwise for every fixed n.
5017: Proof. For one conditional integration, differentiation of the normalized logarithm is the joint-cumulant
5018: identity. The set-partition formula for a derivative of a composition is precisely the Banach-valued Faà di
5019: Bruno formula: every block of the partition is one incoming connected component and the outer cumulant
5020: joins those blocks at the current scale. Fixing the least-label order makes this representation unique.
5021: Induction over the finite sequence of exact conditional integrations proves (115); the terminal matching
5022: logarithm is the final induction step. The weak rooted margin pays every nonroot weak vertex by σ < 1,
5023: while the finite macrobridge and matching KP expansion pay the finitely many terminal vertices. The
5024: one/two-root bounds control a component while its source leaves remain external. These majorants are
5025: summable independently of m, so dominated convergence gives the last assertion.

4993: Exact finite-depth multiscale forest formula For a microscopic depth m, let Rj denote the exact
4994: normalized conditional-logarithm map that integrates the scale-j fluctuation field and returns the source-
4995: decorated effective interaction at scale j − 1. The maps include the regular/irregular partition of unity
4996: before the logarithm is taken, so no all-good denominator occurs. Write
4997: 
4998:                                      Wm (J) = log Zm (J) − log Zm (0)
4999: 
5000: for the normalized finite-volume source logarithm. Iterating the exact maps Rm , . . . , Rjw and then the
5001: finite macrobridge is an identity, not an asymptotic expansion.
5002:    A scale-labelled source forest is generated recursively as follows. A source derivative is a labelled leaf.
5003: At one conditional integration, connected cumulant differentiation joins a nonempty partition of the
5004: incoming leaves and decorated subtrees to one new vertex labelled by that integration scale. Terms
5005: transported without joining retain their existing components. At the terminal matching logarithm, the
5006: remaining components are joined by the matching-scale connected cumulants. The ordering of children is
5007: fixed by the least source label, so isomorphic terms are not counted more than once.
5008: Lemma 8.6 (Exact multiscale Faà di Bruno forest formula). For every finite depth m and every fixed
5009: derivative order n,                                        X
5010:                              Dn Wm (0)[J1 , . . . , Jn ] =   Am (τ ; J1 , . . . , Jn ),                 (115)
5011:                                                       τ ∈Tm,n
5012: 
5013: where Tm,n is the finite family of recursively generated scale-labelled connected trees described above. Each
5014: analytic coefficient of each exact conditional logarithm occurs exactly once. After absolute values are
5015: taken, the sum is dominated uniformly in m by the rooted weak rows and the finite matching expansion.
5016: Hence (115) passes to the infinite weak-depth limit termwise for every fixed n.
5017: Proof. For one conditional integration, differentiation of the normalized logarithm is the joint-cumulant
5018: identity. The set-partition formula for a derivative of a composition is precisely the Banach-valued Faà di
5019: Bruno formula: every block of the partition is one incoming connected component and the outer cumulant
5020: joins those blocks at the current scale. Fixing the least-label order makes this representation unique.
5021: Induction over the finite sequence of exact conditional integrations proves (115); the terminal matching
5022: logarithm is the final induction step. The weak rooted margin pays every nonroot weak vertex by σ < 1,
5023: while the finite macrobridge and matching KP expansion pay the finitely many terminal vertices. The
5024: one/two-root bounds control a component while its source leaves remain external. These majorants are
5025: summable independently of m, so dominated convergence gives the last assertion.

5219: algebra g:
5220: (I1) the invariant symmetric bilinear forms form a one-dimensional space;
5221: (I2) there is no reflection-compatible gauge-invariant local vector of engineering dimension at most four;

5223:      even scalar dimension-four block is generated by the Yang–Mills density;
5224: (I4) the gauge-invariant jet quotient through dimension six is finite. A primitive cubic symmetric color
5225:      tensor occurs precisely for Lie type Ar with r ≥ 2 and is retained in the dimension-six block; its
5226:       presence does not enlarge the dimension-four marginal block.

5228: fixed G after enlarging only the finite jet dimension.

5217: 8.2    Ward identities and Euclidean covariance
5218: Theorem 8.16 (Fixed-group invariant-jet admission through dimension six). For a compact simple Lie
5219: algebra g:
5220: (I1) the invariant symmetric bilinear forms form a one-dimensional space;
5221: (I2) there is no reflection-compatible gauge-invariant local vector of engineering dimension at most four;
5222: (I3) modulo total differences, equations of motion, and the reflection-odd topological density, the reflection-
5223:      even scalar dimension-four block is generated by the Yang–Mills density;
5224: (I4) the gauge-invariant jet quotient through dimension six is finite. A primitive cubic symmetric color
5225:      tensor occurs precisely for Lie type Ar with r ≥ 2 and is retained in the dimension-six block; its
5226:       presence does not enlarge the dimension-four marginal block.
5227: The filtered finite-matrix transport and associated-graded contraction used below therefore apply for every
5228: fixed G after enlarging only the finite jet dimension.
5229: Proof. Simplicity gives the uniqueness of the invariant bilinear form. Semisimplicity makes every finite-
5230: dimensional representation traceless on g. Lorentz index counting, gauge covariance, and reflection parity
5231: then exclude a single free vector index through dimension four and leave only the quadratic curvature
5232: scalar in the even dimension-four sector. Chevalley’s theorem makes the invariant polynomial algebra
5233: finitely generated; the degrees are the exponents plus one. Through degree three a primitive symmetric
5234: tensor exists only for type Ar , and it first multiplies three curvatures, hence has engineering dimension
5235: six. At every fixed engineering cutoff only finitely many covariant derivatives and invariant contractions
5236: occur.

4665: internally nontrivial compact singlet is in AJH .

4802: 7.1    Acoustic exclusion at the exact matching call site
4803: Lemma 7.5 (No massless retained reference at matching). In the exact post-compact joint partition of
4804: Corollary 6.40, the retained compact field has product normalized Haar reference and has already been
4805: integrated coefficientwise before the optical connected logarithm is taken. The massless tangent Feynman
4806: Gaussian used in the weak normal-chart construction is not a live matching reference. The only Gaussian
4807: reference left live is the normalized optical one-core product, and every cross-core optical term is contained
4808: in the explicit inventory AJG .
4809: Proof. The exact compact collect of Theorem 6.39 and the post-compact identity of Corollary 6.40 restore
4810: product Haar on the retained compact variables and place every nonempty retained dependence into the
4811: compact, Gaussian, regular, or irregular activity inventories. The weak tangent Gaussian contributes
4812: no undeclared covariance edge to this matching logarithm. By contrast, the optical one-core references
4813: remain live, while their off-diagonal quadratic blocks and other cross-core terms occur explicitly in AJG
4814: and retain the spatial payment used in Theorem 6.43.
4815: Lemma 7.6 (Vacuum factorization after the complete owner map). Fix the optical variables and expand
4816: one compact coefficient in Peter–Weyl sectors. Suppose every compact cut separating two declared root
4817: buffers carries the trivial representation. Remove only the cross-core objects already registered in the
4818: four disjoint inventories of Corollary 6.40. If no registered connector remains between the roots, the
4819: residual coefficient factorizes into one-core expectations and empty-support scalars. Its contribution to the
4820: normalized connected two-root logarithm is zero.
4821: Proof. Triviality on every separating compact cut permits repeated Haar–Fubini separation. The exact
4822: incidence-split collect retains every internally nontrivial singlet in AJH , while the owner map assigns every
4823: other cross-core occurrence to exactly one of AJG , AJreg , AJirr , or AJH . After those registered objects are
4824: removed, the residual history has no object joining distinct one-core components. It therefore factorizes.
4825: Empty-support scalars are removed by the normalization, and products of separate one-root expectations
4826: are removed by the connected logarithm.
4827: Theorem 7.7 (Exact acoustic exclusion-or-capture). Consider any connected zero-, one-, or two-source
4828: coefficient joining two bounded matching-scale root buffers in the exact joint logarithm. At least one object
4829: on the unique root-to-root forest path belongs to one of the following classes:
4830:    (i) a nonvacuum compact activity controlled by Theorem 6.39 and the spatial row (99);
4831:   (ii) an optical covariance bond or an optical Gaussian cross-core activity in AJG ;
4832:  (iii) a registered reference, regular, irregular, seam, reflection, wrapping, normalization, determinant, or
4833:        source connector with its original support payment.
4834: There is no fourth class consisting of a massless retained acoustic reference edge.
4835: Proof. Assume that a connected coefficient contains no object of types (i)–(iii). Absence of (i) makes
4836: every separating compact cut vacuum. Absence of (iii) leaves no registered physical connector after the
4837: complete owner map. Lemma 7.6 then reduces the compact contribution to disconnected one-core factors
4838: and scalars. Absence of (ii) also prevents the still-live optical field from joining the roots. The coefficient
4839: is therefore disconnected, a contradiction. The only possible remaining concern would be an undeclared
4840: massless retained covariance, which is excluded by Lemma 7.5.
4841:

4844: the boundary-to-center estimates (104) and (107) require no additional terminal Witten, acoustic-channel,
4845: or retained-Gaussian gap hypothesis.
4846: Proof. The vertex inventory is already exhaustive by Lemma 6.7, Proposition 6.8, and Corollary 6.40.
4847: Apply Theorem 7.7 to exclude the only remaining acoustic fourth-class concern.

5958: Osterwalder–Schrader continuation Let Dloc be the local field-polynomial test algebra. The O(4)
5959: covariance proved above is an identity of the Euclidean Schwinger system. Rotations preserving the
5960: chosen time-zero hyperplane act directly on the positive-time quotient; rotations mixing Euclidean time

5962: Theorem 8.46 (Euclidean covariance and Poincaré reconstruction). The Schwinger family satisfies the
5963: full O(4) covariance axiom. Together with reflection positivity, permutation symmetry, temperedness
5964: and continuous translations, this gives by the Osterwalder–Schrader reconstruction theorem a strongly
5965: continuous unitary representation of the proper orthochronous Poincaré group on the reconstructed Hilbert
5966: space, with translation spectrum in the closed forward cone. Spatial rotations and translations are obtained
5967: directly on the OS quotient; Lorentz boosts arise from analytic continuation of the Euclidean rotation
5968: covariance.
5969: Proof. Theorem 8.45 is precisely the Euclidean covariance identity required for every finite tensor/source
5970: multiplet, and the nested source construction extends it to the complete chosen local field family. The
5971: direct OS theorem, Lemma 8.14, and Lemma 8.13 supply the remaining reconstruction hypotheses,
5972: including the linear-growth condition. The standard Osterwalder–Schrader covariance reconstruction
5973: then produces the positive- energy Poincaré representation. No unitary action of a Euclidean rotation
5974: that changes the reflection hyperplane is assumed on the intermediate OS quotient.

6056: Proof. At the Gaussian fixed point the centered scalar curvature composite creates a two-curvature Fock
6057: vector. In Fourier variables its squared norm is an integral of the squared symmetrized two-particle
6058: amplitude against the positive transverse curvature spectral density. That density is nonzero on an open
6059: set, so choosing a positive-time profile whose Fourier transform is nonzero there gives a strictly positive
6060: integral. Reflection positivity identifies the integral with the displayed OS form. Scale covariance of the
6061: tangent-normalized source makes the value independent of r.

3235: Lemma 5.21 (Finite graded curvature-jet quotient). Fix an engineering cutoff D. Modulo vacuum
3236: constants, total lattice differences, gauge and Bianchi identities, equation-of-motion/redundant directions,

3243: The same remains true after adjoining any fixed finite collection of retained, source, displacement, profile,
3244: stress, rotation-defect, and one/two-root marks.
3245: Proof. After a boundary-rooted tree gauge, one enlarged block cell contains a finite alphabet of Lie-algebra
3246: variables and difference labels. Jets of bounded engineering dimension form a finite polynomial span.
3247: Every listed quotient relation is linear on that span, and adjoining finitely many marks gives a finite
3248: direct sum.
3249: Lemma 5.22 (No reflection-even scalar of engineering dimension five). In four-dimensional pure fixed-G
3250: lattice gauge theory, the signed-hypercubic and reflection-even gauge-invariant scalar jet quotient has no
3251: engineering-dimension-five component.
3252: Proof. A nonvacuum dimension-five scalar would have to contain two curvature factors and one covariant
3253: difference, hence five coordinate indices before contraction. The invariant tensors of the signed hypercubic
3254: group are generated by even-rank Kronecker contractions and the rank-four alternating tensor. They
3255: cannot contract an odd total of five indices to a scalar without a preferred vector. Total differences and
3256: Bianchi descendants are already removed in the quotient. Thus the reflection-even scalar sector jumps
3257: from the extracted dimension-four Yang–Mills density to dimension at least six.
3258: 
3259: Lemma 5.23 (No reflection-even rank-two curvature jet of dimension five). In the local gauge-invariant
3260: rank-two curvature-jet quotient relevant to the stress/displacement source, the reflection-even engineering-
3261: dimension-five component is zero.
3262: Proof. A dimension-five curvature bilinear has the schematic Lorentz-index content F DF , hence five
3263: tensor indices before contractions. To leave exactly two free rank-two indices one would have to contract
3264: an odd set of three indices. The signed-hypercubic invariant tensors available in the reflection-even
3265: sector are products of Kronecker deltas and pairs of Levi–Civita tensors, all of even rank. A single
3266: Levi–Civita tensor is reflection odd. Thus no reflection-even constant coefficient tensor can perform the
3267: required contraction. Terms in which the derivative is moved onto the whole bilinear are total differences,
3268: while Bianchi and equation-of-motion descendants are already quotiented. The next possible rank-two
3269: complement after dimension four therefore begins at dimension six.

3243: The same remains true after adjoining any fixed finite collection of retained, source, displacement, profile,
3244: stress, rotation-defect, and one/two-root marks.
3245: Proof. After a boundary-rooted tree gauge, one enlarged block cell contains a finite alphabet of Lie-algebra
3246: variables and difference labels. Jets of bounded engineering dimension form a finite polynomial span.
3247: Every listed quotient relation is linear on that span, and adjoining finitely many marks gives a finite
3248: direct sum.
3249: Lemma 5.22 (No reflection-even scalar of engineering dimension five). In four-dimensional pure fixed-G
3250: lattice gauge theory, the signed-hypercubic and reflection-even gauge-invariant scalar jet quotient has no
3251: engineering-dimension-five component.
3252: Proof. A nonvacuum dimension-five scalar would have to contain two curvature factors and one covariant
3253: difference, hence five coordinate indices before contraction. The invariant tensors of the signed hypercubic
3254: group are generated by even-rank Kronecker contractions and the rank-four alternating tensor. They
3255: cannot contract an odd total of five indices to a scalar without a preferred vector. Total differences and
3256: Bianchi descendants are already removed in the quotient. Thus the reflection-even scalar sector jumps
3257: from the extracted dimension-four Yang–Mills density to dimension at least six.
3258: 
3259: Lemma 5.23 (No reflection-even rank-two curvature jet of dimension five). In the local gauge-invariant
3260: rank-two curvature-jet quotient relevant to the stress/displacement source, the reflection-even engineering-
3261: dimension-five component is zero.
3262: Proof. A dimension-five curvature bilinear has the schematic Lorentz-index content F DF , hence five
3263: tensor indices before contractions. To leave exactly two free rank-two indices one would have to contract
3264: an odd set of three indices. The signed-hypercubic invariant tensors available in the reflection-even
3265: sector are products of Kronecker deltas and pairs of Levi–Civita tensors, all of even rank. A single
3266: Levi–Civita tensor is reflection odd. Thus no reflection-even constant coefficient tensor can perform the
3267: required contraction. Terms in which the derivative is moved onto the whole bilinear are total differences,
3268: while Bianchi and equation-of-motion descendants are already quotiented. The next possible rank-two
3269: complement after dimension four therefore begins at dimension six.

3202: Theorem 5.19 (Exact fiber-chart invariance). Let Ψ1 (w, z1 ) and Ψ2 (w, z2 ) be two exact C 1 fiber charts
3203: for the same finite-dimensional submersion B(U ) = w. Suppose their images agree on the integration
3204: region and their transition map is orientation preserving. Then, for every integrable density F ,
3205:                       Z                                   Z
3206:                          F (Ψ1 (w, z1 )) J1 (w, z1 ) dz1 = F (Ψ2 (w, z2 )) J2 (w, z2 ) dz2 .

3208: Consequently the normalized conditional expectation, its connected logarithm, and every background
3209: derivative of the complete local standard-scheme coordinate and its quadratic coefficient are independent
3210: of the chosen exact fiber chart.
3211: 
3212: 
3213:                                                         48
3214: 
3215: Proof. The first identity is the ordinary change-of-variables theorem on the fiber. Division by the same
3216: fiber mass gives equality of normalized conditional expectations. Connected logarithms are functions
3217: of those expectations. Differentiation is permitted on any common dominated marked domain. The
3218: determinant statement follows because the two Jacobians are related by the determinant of the transition
3219: map, which cancels under the change of variables. It must not be inserted a second time.

5229: Proof. Simplicity gives the uniqueness of the invariant bilinear form. Semisimplicity makes every finite-
5230: dimensional representation traceless on g. Lorentz index counting, gauge covariance, and reflection parity
5231: then exclude a single free vector index through dimension four and leave only the quadratic curvature
5232: scalar in the even dimension-four sector. Chevalley’s theorem makes the invariant polynomial algebra
5233: finitely generated; the degrees are the exponents plus one. Through degree three a primitive symmetric
5234: tensor exists only for type Ar , and it first multiplies three curvatures, hence has engineering dimension
5235: six. At every fixed engineering cutoff only finitely many covariant derivatives and invariant contractions
5236: occur.

5231: then exclude a single free vector index through dimension four and leave only the quadratic curvature
5232: scalar in the even dimension-four sector. Chevalley’s theorem makes the invariant polynomial algebra
5233: finitely generated; the degrees are the exponents plus one. Through degree three a primitive symmetric
5234: tensor exists only for type Ar , and it first multiplies three curvatures, hence has engineering dimension
5235: six. At every fixed engineering cutoff only finitely many covariant derivatives and invariant contractions
5236: occur.

6034:                                                H Ω⊥ ≥ m > 0.                                          (169)

6036: The vacuum is unique.
6037: Proof. The centered classes [F ] − S(F )Ω, F ∈ D+ , are dense in Ω⊥ . Lemma 9.2 says that the spectral
6038: projection EH ([0, m − ε]) annihilates this dense set for every ε > 0; hence it vanishes on Ω⊥ . This proves
6039: the absence of spectrum in (0, m).
6040:    For a centered core vector, (168) tends to zero, so its spectral measure has no atom at zero. Density
6041: then shows that EH ({0})H = CΩ. Thus the vacuum is unique and (169) holds.

6044: 10.1     Endpoint interpolation and reflected noncollapse
6045: Let Yr (f ) be the tangent-normalized, Wick-centered scalar curvature source at physical scale r, with the
6046: source root parallel transported in the normalized finite-jet frame. The test profile f is chosen once at
6047: unit scale and dilated with the source so that its dimensionless support remains fixed and lies strictly in
6048: positive Euclidean time.
6049: Lemma 10.1 (Positive Gaussian OS norm). There is such a profile f for which
6050: 
6051:                                     V0 (f ) := S0 ΘYr (f ) Yr (f ) > 0.
6052:                                                                   
6053: 
6054: 
6055: The value is independent of r in the normalized frame.
6056: Proof. At the Gaussian fixed point the centered scalar curvature composite creates a two-curvature Fock
6057: vector. In Fourier variables its squared norm is an integral of the squared symmetrized two-particle
6058: amplitude against the positive transverse curvature spectral density. That density is nonzero on an open
6059: set, so choosing a positive-time profile whose Fourier transform is nonzero there gives a strictly positive
6060: integral. Reflection positivity identifies the integral with the displayed OS form. Scale covariance of the
6061: tangent-normalized source makes the value independent of r.

6065:                                                    √
6066: physical weak scale r, put ur = u(r) and gr = ur , and use g rather than u = g 2 as the interpolation
6067: parameter. For the complete finite-volume normalized reflected two-root functional, and also with one
6068: fixed finite defect root, the following hold on the full segment 0 ≤ g ≤ gr :
6069:     (i) the graph-segment identities are C 2 and exact before every limit;
6070:    (ii) the stable/wrapping orbit rows, the pre-owner two-mark compact entrance row, and the terminal
6071:         packet-shell row have constants independent of cutoff, volume, preceding weak depth, crossing cell,
6072:         and terminal depth M ;
6073:   (iii) differentiation is performed inside each fixed crossing cell before ownership, never on the crossing
6074:         index, and the resulting total derivatives extend across cell boundaries;
6075:   (iv) the no-crossing terminal remainder tends to zero through two g-derivatives uniformly as M → ∞.
